| counter-proof: a mail whose text names no one gives only the people of its headers; a mail with neither gives nothing | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| the headers give every address and every display name that is given: a null or blank name gives none, an address is kept as given | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| a mail address in the text is found whole; a sentence's closing period is not part of it; text that is no address is not found | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| an address given in the headers and found again in the text, in another case, is listed once as the header gave it | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| a run of seven digits or more is a phone number, with the separators it was written with and a leading + | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| a run of six digits is no phone number, nor are several short runs a blank or a line break apart, nor a run that a letter touches | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| the same phone number written two ways is listed once, as first found | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| an @ followed by letters, digits, _, - or . is an account handle, listed with its @ and without a closing period | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| an @ inside a mail address starts no handle; a lone @ and an @ before a blank are no handle | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| the words after a greeting at the start of a line, up to the first comma or the end of the line, are a name | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| an optional title after the greeting is not part of the name: Mr, Ms, Herr, Frau and Dr, with or without a period | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| a greeting's case does not matter | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| no name is given by a greeting that is not at the start of a line, one inside a longer word, or one with nothing but a comma after it | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| the line after a closing is a name, for each listed closing, whatever its case | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| no name is given by a line that only starts like a closing, a closing with no line after it, a blank line after it, or a line with no letter | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| the first line of the signature, after a line of two hyphens and a blank, is a name; its other lines are not | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| no name is given by two hyphens without the blank, three hyphens, a signature line inside a line, or nothing after the line | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| a name found in the headers and again by a greeting, a closing and a signature, in other cases, is listed once as first found | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| peopleIn and personHits together: the text of a mail's sender names the sender, and the same text without the people passes | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| it is pure: the same result twice, the mail unchanged, and no request sent | unit | undeclared |  |  |

| a whole name is found with its kind, its text as written, its line and its column; the same text without it gives none | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| a name is found whatever its case, and the hit holds the text as it stands | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| a part of a name is found by itself: the last part at column 6, the first part at column 7 | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| a part of a name is kept as written, a period included: Dr. is a part of Dr. Ada Example | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| a name is matched as text, not as a pattern: C++ is found as C++, and A.B is not found in AxB | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| where a part and the whole name are both found at the same place, the whole name is the one hit | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| a person listed twice is one hit | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| a word that only contains a name part is no hit: Annabel, Hanna and Anna2 for Anna, and Müllerstraße for Müller | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| an address is found whole, whatever its case, with its kind, line and column | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| the local part of an address is found by itself; a longer word with it inside is no hit | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| a local part inside the whole address is not a hit of its own: the address is the one hit | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| a phone number is found by its digits whatever the separators: hyphens and a slash, or blanks, parentheses and a dot | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| a phone number after other digits in the same run is found: a ticket number before it, on line 1 and on line 2 | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| only whole digit groups of a run count: 4712 345678 is no hit of 12 345678, nor is a stretch of other digits | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| a phone number with other digits is no hit, nor a run that a letter touches, nor a run that a line break splits | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| an account is found with and without the leading @, whichever way it is given; a longer word with it inside is no hit | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| line and column count from 1: the second line's hit, after a line feed and after a carriage return with a line feed | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| hits are given by line, then column, whatever the order in which the people are listed | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| hits at the same place are given in the order name, address, phone, account | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| a text that names none of the people gives an empty list, and so do no people, an empty text and empty entries | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| it is pure: the same result twice, the people unchanged, and no request sent | unit | undeclared |  |  |

| ADDING A PRODUCT CREATES ITS LAYOUT — only what is missing | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| the decision row of an accepted entry is the row of the decision table | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| replaceSection writes the proposal byte for byte, every other byte unchanged | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| an entry already written in the queue's decisions is not written twice | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| A GITLAB PRODUCT USES A PROJECT ACCESS TOKEN — the settings page takes whether a token may write from the host | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| A STALE APPROVAL IS NOT APPLIED — the engine's workflow route: a proposal or a SPEC section changed after approval writes nothing and is named | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| WITHOUT A TOKEN, THE INSTANCE'S WORKFLOW WRITES THE CHANGE — the engine writes what the dashboard's acceptance commit writes, entry by entry in the queue's order | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| WITHOUT A TOKEN, THE INSTANCE'S WORKFLOW WRITES THE CHANGE — a second run writes nothing; a record the dashboard already applied is passed over | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| A STALE APPROVAL IS NOT APPLIED — a malformed record is refused and named, and nothing of it is written | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| the records are read from the approvals folder: README.md, other kinds and files that are no Markdown are passed over | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| front matter: scalars and lists, body separated | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| front matter with CR LF line ends: the fields of the LF text, the body the text's own bytes | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| a reviewed file's identifier from its path — the same for a renamed file | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| AN EDITED FILE KEEPS ITS IDENTIFIER — a text with CR LF line ends declares the identifier of its LF text | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| EACH REMOTE SESSION HAS ITS OWN PORT FROM THE CONFIGURED RANGE — the lowest free port; a full range refuses and says so | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| THE DASHBOARD WRITES THE TUNNEL COMMANDS — both ends filled from the settings, matching each other | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| the jump host's settings are checked — a host, user, key name or range that could change the command is refused | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| A REVERSE TUNNEL LISTENS ONLY ON THE JUMP HOST'S LOOPBACK — the writer refuses a command whose forward binds elsewhere | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| the app never calls fetch directly — every request goes through an adapter | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| the kernel never reaches a repository — no kernel file imports anything of MOD-repository-hosts | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| the dashboard reaches MOD-repository-hosts only through its interface — no file of the dashboard imports another of its files | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| EVERY STEP EXPLAINS ITSELF — a step without an explanation cannot be rendered | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| every site module parses — the app itself is only run in a browser, so check its syntax here | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| the list of the person's tokens is GitHub's page of fine-grained tokens, where a token is renewed | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| the use-case key is `area` — `stage` is used nowhere in the dashboard's own files | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| A USED-UP RATE LIMIT IS NAMED — the load error names the account's limit and when it resets, and nothing about the token | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| A USED-UP RATE LIMIT IS NAMED — without a token, the network's limit and when it resets | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| A USED-UP RATE LIMIT IS NAMED — a refused write names the limit; counter-proof: a 403 without the headers is a missing permission | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| A USED-UP RATE LIMIT IS NAMED — a GitLab product's limit is named by its server, without a time | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| AN EXPIRED TOKEN IS NAMED AND ITS RENEWAL LINKED — the load error still names a refused token and links its renewal | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| the dashboard is opened on a GitLab product by its address; its files link to GitLab | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| A REVIEWED ARTIFACT ENTERS THE DEFAULT BRANCH AS OPEN — every kind is open until an acceptance commit names its text, and an edit committed afterwards is not accepted | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| A GENERATED ARTIFACT IS A PROPOSAL — a file is accepted by a record naming its current text, never by one naming another text or another file | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| A RECORD IS EVIDENCE, NOT A PROPOSAL — a job record without approval is not listed as open; counter-proof: a use case without approval is | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| A RECORD IS EVIDENCE, NOT A PROPOSAL — an acceptance that names a job, gate or approval record writes no approval record | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| PSEUDONYMISATION IS ON UNLESS A PRODUCT SWITCHES IT OFF — docs/settings.md, its key in the front matter | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| A PERSON IS NAMED BY ACCOUNT OR WITH CONSENT — docs/collaborators.md reads and writes losslessly; a row records an agreement | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| A USED-UP RATE LIMIT IS NAMED — a 403 with X-RateLimit-Remaining: 0 and X-RateLimit-Limit: 5000 is the account's limit, with its reset time | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| A USED-UP RATE LIMIT IS NAMED — GitHub's 429 is the same limit | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| A USED-UP RATE LIMIT IS NAMED — without a token, X-RateLimit-Limit: 60 is the network's limit | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| A USED-UP RATE LIMIT IS NAMED — counter-proof: a 403 without those headers is still a missing permission, not a limit | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| A USED-UP RATE LIMIT IS NAMED — a refused write is named the same, and nothing is written | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| A USED-UP RATE LIMIT IS NAMED — a GitLab product's 429 is its limit, named without a time; a 403 there is not | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| AN EXPIRED TOKEN IS NAMED AND ITS RENEWAL LINKED — a 401 stays a refused token, rate-limit headers or not | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| ONE GITHUB TOKEN SERVES EVERY FEATURE — the new token's page asks for Contents, Issues, Pull requests, Actions, Workflows, Secrets write and Metadata read | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| A GITLAB PRODUCT USES A PROJECT ACCESS TOKEN — the project's own Access tokens page; GitHub's list of the person's tokens | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| NO TEXT TRAVELS IN A URL — the new-file page carries a path and a record; GitLab has no web fallback | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| A PRODUCT IS NAMED BY ITS ADDRESS — on GitHub and on a GitLab server, as copied from the browser | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| A TOKEN GOES ONLY TO THE SERVER THAT ISSUED IT — the GitHub token only to GitHub's API, the project token only to its project's API | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| A SAVE IS REFUSED WHEN THE TEXT CHANGED MEANWHILE — a commit on a head that moved is Moved, and nothing is written | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| AN EXPIRED TOKEN IS NAMED AND ITS RENEWAL LINKED — a refused request yields the token's name and the renewal link | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| GITLAB PRODUCTS ARE SUPPORTED — reading at the pinned commit, every page of the tree, and one commit of several actions | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| THE DASHBOARD KEEPS ITS PRODUCTS IN THE BROWSER — adding a product stores its address in localStorage and commits nothing; counter-proof: after a clear the list is empty | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| A product's own token is kept under its own key, beside the instance's, and goes with a clear of that key | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| SETTINGS ARE EXPORTED AND IMPORTED WITH THEIR SECRETS — an import of an export restores every setting, secrets included; counter-proof: nothing is sent | unit | undeclared |  |  |
| AN EXPORT CAN BE LOCKED WITH A PASSPHRASE — no secret in clear, imports with it, a wrong one imports nothing | unit | undeclared |  |  |

| THE JUMP HOST AND THE REMOTE SESSIONS ARE SETTINGS — stored under their keys, exported and imported, cleared by a clear | unit | undeclared |  |  |
